PREAMBLE
The goals of the Open Font License (OFL) are to stimulate worldwide
development of collaborative font projects, to support the font creation
efforts of academic and linguistic communities, and to provide a free and
open framework in which fonts may be shared and improved in partnership
with others.

The OFL allows the licensed fonts to be used, studied, modified and
redistributed freely as long as they are not sold by themselves. The
fonts, including any derivative works, can be bundled, embedded,
redistributed and/or sold with any software provided that any reserved
names are not used by derivative works. The fonts and derivatives,
however, cannot be released under any other type of license. The
requirement for fonts to remain under this license does not apply
to any document created using the fonts or their derivatives.

DEFINITIONS
"Font Software" refers to the set of files released by the Copyright
Holder(s) under this license and clearly marked as such. This may
include source files, build scripts and documentation.

"Reserved Font Name" refers to any names specified as such after the
copyright statement(s).

"Original Version" refers to the collection of Font Software components as
distributed by the Copyright Holder(s).

"Modified Version" refers to any derivative made by adding to, deleting,
or substituting -- in part or in whole -- any of the components of the
Original Version, by changing formats or by porting the Font Software to a
new environment.

"Author" refers to any designer, engineer, programmer, technical
writer or other person who contributed to the Font Software.

PERMISSION & CONDITIONS
Permission is hereby granted, free of charge, to any person obtaining
a copy of the Font Software, to use, study, copy, merge, embed, modify,
redistribute, and sell modified and unmodified copies of the Font
Software, subject to the following conditions:

1) Neither the Font Software nor any of its individual components,
in Original or Modified Versions, may be sold by itself.

2) Original or Modified Versions of the Font Software may be bundled,
redistributed and/or sold with any software, provided that each copy
contains the above copyright notice and this license. These can be
included either as stand-alone text files, human-readable headers or
in the appropriate machine-readable metadata fields within text or
binary files as long as those fields can be easily viewed by the user.

3) No Modified Version of the Font Software may use the Reserved Font
Name(s) unless explicit written permission is granted by the corresponding
Copyright Holder. This restriction only applies to the primary font name as
presented to the users.

4) The name(s) of the Copyright Holder(s) or the Author(s) of the Font
Software shall not be used to promote, endorse or advertise any
Modified Version, except to acknowledge the contribution(s) of the
Copyright Holder(s) and the Author(s) or with their explicit written
permission.

5) The Font Software, modified or unmodified, in part or in whole,
must be distributed entirely under this license, and must not be
distributed under any other license. The requirement for fonts to
remain under this license does not apply to any document created
using the Font Software.

TERMINATION
This license becomes null and void if any of the above conditions are
not met.

SQLite Is Public Domain

All of the code and documentation in SQLite has been dedicated to the public
domain by the authors. All code authors, and representatives of the companies
they work for, have signed affidavits dedicating their contributions to the
public domain and originals of those signed affidavits are stored in a firesafe
at the main offices of Hwaci. Anyone is free to copy, modify, publish, use,
compile, sell, or distribute the original SQLite code, either in source code
form or as a compiled binary, for any purpose, commercial or non-commercial,
and by any means.

The previous paragraph applies to the deliverable code and documentation in
SQLite - those parts of the SQLite library that you actually bundle and ship
with a larger application. Some scripts used as part of the build process (for
example the "configure" scripts generated by autoconf) might fall under other
open-source licenses. Nothing from these build scripts ever reaches the final
deliverable SQLite library, however, and so the licenses associated with those
scripts should not be a factor in assessing your rights to copy and use the
SQLite library.

All of the deliverable code in SQLite has been written from scratch. No code
has been taken from other projects or from the open internet. Every line of
code can be traced back to its original author, and all of those authors have
public domain dedications on file. So the SQLite code base is clean and is
uncontaminated with licensed code from other projects.

THIRD-PARTY COMPONENTS
===============================================================================

This software bundles or links the components below. Each is distributed under
its own license, reproduced in full further down this file.

  ExecuTorch          BSD 3-Clause   native runtime and hardware backends
  pffft / FFTPACK     BSD 3-Clause   legacy/cpp/pfft, audio FFT
  OpenCV              Apache-2.0     vision pipelines and multimodal LLMs
  KleidiCV            Apache-2.0     OpenCV Arm HAL, Android only
  ada                 MIT            legacy/cpp/ada, URL parsing
  phonemis            MIT            grapheme-to-phoneme for text-to-speech
  MLX                 MIT            MLX backend, iOS only

Which of these end up in an application binary depends on the backends and
libraries it enables; see the Native Libraries documentation. ExecuTorch in
turn bundles further components, among them XNNPACK, pthreadpool, cpuinfo,
the tokenizers library and PCRE2, each under its own permissive license and
covered by the notices shipped with the ExecuTorch distribution.

# Third-party notices

The source code of Phonemis is licensed under the MIT license (see [LICENSE](LICENSE)). Some data files in
[`data/`](data/) and some bundled libraries in [`src/third-party/`](src/third-party/) come from other projects and
are distributed under their own terms, listed below. The full texts of those licenses are in
[`licenses/`](licenses/).

## Data

### English lexicons

- **Files:** `data/en-us/lexicon_full.json`, `data/en-us/lexicon_small.json`, `data/en-gb/lexicon_full.json`,
  `data/en-gb/lexicon_small.json`
- **Source:** the gold and silver pronunciation dictionaries of [misaki](https://github.com/hexgrad/misaki)
  by hexgrad.
- **License:** Apache License 2.0, see [`licenses/misaki-Apache-2.0.txt`](licenses/misaki-Apache-2.0.txt).
- **Changes:** the dictionaries were modified for Phonemis:
  - flattened, so that a word's part-of-speech variants become separate `word|TAG` entries
    ([`scripts/tools/create_lexicon.py`](scripts/tools/create_lexicon.py));
  - the gold and silver dictionaries were merged into one file
    ([`scripts/tools/concatenate_lexicons.py`](scripts/tools/concatenate_lexicons.py));
  - the small lexicons are reduced versions of the full ones.

### English part-of-speech tagger

- **Files:** `data/en-us/tagger.json`, `data/en-gb/tagger.json` (and the tag descriptions in `tags.json`)
- **Source:** HMM probabilities computed by [`scripts/tools/create_tagger.py`](scripts/tools/create_tagger.py) from
  the sentences of the [Brown Corpus](http://www.hit.uib.no/icame/brown/bcm.html) (W. N. Francis and H. Kučera, as
  distributed with NLTK), tagged with [spaCy](https://spacy.io).
- **License:** NLTK lists the Brown Corpus as *"May be used for non-commercial purposes."* Treat these files as
  **not cleared for commercial use**.
- **Commercial use:** the tagger is optional. Leave out `Config::tagger`, and English is phonemized without it,
  with weaker handling of words whose pronunciation depends on their part of speech (*read*, *used to*). A tagger
  trained on commercially usable text is planned.

### Neural phonemizer models

- **Files:** `data/*/phonemizer_*.bin`
- **Source:** Protophone models trained by the Phonemis authors on pronunciations generated with
  [misaki](https://github.com/hexgrad/misaki): its gold and silver dictionaries for English and, for the other
  languages, its espeak-ng backend ([espeak-ng](https://github.com/espeak-ng/espeak-ng) is licensed under GPL-3.0).
- **License:** MIT, as the rest of Phonemis. The models contain only weights learned from those pronunciations: no
  code or dictionaries of misaki or espeak-ng.

### Mandarin lexicon

- **Files:** `data/zh/lexicon.json`
- **Source:** built by [`scripts/tools/create_zh_lexicon.py`](scripts/tools/create_zh_lexicon.py): the phonemes
  [misaki](https://github.com/hexgrad/misaki) gives (its Kokoro v1.0 Mandarin G2P) for every Han character and phrase
  of the dictionaries of [pypinyin](https://github.com/mozillazg/python-pinyin) by mozillazg.
- **License:** misaki: Apache License 2.0, see [`licenses/misaki-Apache-2.0.txt`](licenses/misaki-Apache-2.0.txt);
  pypinyin: MIT, see [`licenses/pypinyin-MIT.txt`](licenses/pypinyin-MIT.txt).

### Mandarin word segmentation

- **Files:** `data/zh/segmenter.json`
- **Source:** the default dictionary and the HMM probabilities of [jieba](https://github.com/fxsjy/jieba) by Sun
  Junyi, exported by [`scripts/tools/create_zh_segmenter.py`](scripts/tools/create_zh_segmenter.py).
- **License:** MIT, see [`licenses/jieba-MIT.txt`](licenses/jieba-MIT.txt).

## Ported code

`src/phonemis/lang/zh/num2word.cpp` reads numbers as [cn2an](https://github.com/Ailln/cn2an) by Ailln does: a C++ port
of its Arabic-to-Chinese conversion (`an2cn`). MIT, see [`licenses/cn2an-MIT.txt`](licenses/cn2an-MIT.txt).

## Bundled libraries

| Library | Location | License |
|---|---|---|
| [JSON for Modern C++](https://github.com/nlohmann/json) 3.12.0, by Niels Lohmann | `src/third-party/json.hpp` | MIT, see [`licenses/nlohmann-json-MIT.txt`](licenses/nlohmann-json-MIT.txt) |
| [xsimd](https://github.com/xtensor-stack/xsimd) 14.2, by Johan Mabille, Sylvain Corlay, Wolf Vollprecht, Martin Renou, QuantStack and Serge Guelton | `src/third-party/xsimd/` | BSD 3-Clause, see [`licenses/xsimd-BSD-3-Clause.txt`](licenses/xsimd-BSD-3-Clause.txt) |

## Unicode data

`src/phonemis/utils/unicode_table.h` is generated by
[`scripts/tools/generate_unicode_table.py`](scripts/tools/generate_unicode_table.py) from Python's Unicode database,
which is derived from the Unicode Character Database. Copyright © 1991-2026 Unicode, Inc., distributed under the
Unicode License v3, see [`licenses/Unicode-3.0.txt`](licenses/Unicode-3.0.txt).
